Watch: a double-line brigade with cavalry puts it on both ends of its second line (the player's brigades too); brigade batteries move up to 150 m to a crest in an interval once the brigade holds (`TacticalBatterySite` rows `moved=true reason=crest-interval`, or `kept` with the reason), never ahead of the front line, and stay there when the brigade wheels or re-forms in place (no battery limbering back and forth); a cavalry node takes an outer lane. **Absent:** `tactical-formation:cavalry-flip-il-unmatched`.
   42. **BC14, corps-level mirror (step 0 decides).** `TacticalLayoutMirror` rows per group order (deployment placements included, read from the placed positions; `immediate=true` on those rows): `jq -r 'select(.event=="TacticalLayoutMirror") | [.fields.depth,.fields.brigades,.fields.agree,.fields.reversed,.fields.immediate] | @tsv'`. Rule (BC14 decision 9): with at least 10 rows at each depth, A4 is built when at least 80 % of depth-1-2 brigades agree and at least 80 % of depth-3 brigades are reversed (or the other way round); depth 3 matching depths 1-2 at 80 % closes BUG-TAC-026 as not reproduced; fewer than 10 rows at a depth, or neither pattern at 80 %, is recorded as inconclusive and A4 stays unbuilt (this item stays open). Record the rows in the step-2 commit. After A4: all depths agree, `[once:tactical-formation:corps-mirror-il] [CorpsMirrorFix] rewrote 1 instruction`, and the order preview drawn when the player gives a corps order matches where the regiments go. **Absent:** `tactical-formation:corps-mirror-il-unmatched`.
   43. **BC14, lanes.** `[TacticalCommandAssignment]` rows carry `lane` and `lateralRank`: lanes follow the nodes' left-to-right order on the front axis; watch that no two divisions cross each other to reach their lanes, cavalry holds the outer lanes and the flank guard, and the main effort goes to the better of two equally strong divisions.
3. **Session B — W&L brigade career battle (W&L forces `ai_vs_ai`).**
   1. `[BattlePerception] alliance=<player alliance> mode=OwnSideWithinFog reason=None …` for the player's AI side, FogGrid for the enemy.
   2. No Whiskey write on a player regiment: the player's units never move, rotate, change stance or formation on their own; `TacticalCommandPosture` rows never name a player unit with `writeResult=submitted`.
   3. When the AI moves the player's parent division, a dispatch panel names the superior (one per executed move, no spam).
   4. `[once:tactical-battle-core:ancestry-mismatch] [BattleSnapshot] parent map and Transform.IsChildOf disagree on the player's chain: alliance=<a> groups=<n>` absent, or explained.
   5. Detached command (BC3): with the player's command detached, its AI parent is ordered and rotated in place, and none of the player's regiments is written. Then, with the AI parent holding a stance (`ai_stanceordered > 0`; `ChangeStance` sets the parent's own `permanentlydetached`, 90788-90791), press the player's stance-off (re-attach): the parent is no longer ordered as a group — its children are ordered instead, or a `TacticalDecisionMatrix` row carries the JSON field `wlGuard` = `deny:owned-descendant-attached` (`jq -c 'select(.event=="TacticalDecisionMatrix" and .fields.wlGuard=="deny:owned-descendant-attached")' "$SESSION"/tactical*.jsonl`).
   6. Feud: `[first-fire] tactical.patch.feud-action-gate`; a feud move goes through the executor; **absent:** the no-snapshot feud rows (`tactical-feud-guard:no-snapshot`, `no-snapshot-refused`). The sidecar keeps only the event name and `k=v` fields, and those two rows are the `TacticalFeudGuard` rows without a `reason` field: `jq -c 'select(.event=="TacticalFeudGuard" and .fields.reason==null)' "$SESSION"/tactical*.jsonl` prints nothing. A player-owned feuding group never moves on its own.
   7. **BC10, W&L deployment and arrivals.** Day 1: the player's command and its AI group stand together where vanilla placed them (the command's AI group is never re-placed and no `TacDeployTerrainAdvice` or `TacticalDeploymentPlan` placement moves it; check the group's position against the log of vanilla's placement); no deployment reserve is taken from the direct child that holds the player's command. A player-side reinforcement column: `TacticalArrivalRepath` (step A) only measures, it writes nothing, so the player's own units are never shielded or re-ordered by Whiskey.
   8. **BC11, vanilla flank moves and the player (watch only).** Vanilla's `CheckFlankMoves` / `FlankMove` and `AssignReserves` have no W&L test (finding 8). Watch for the player's regiments moving with no order of the player's while the player's parent group makes a vanilla flank move (`lastaiorder` 4/5; the 45° two-leg hook) or is linked to a flank. Record any occurrence (unit, time) for a later fix; it needs the user's approval because the only remedy is a Prefix on `FlankMove`.
   9. **BC14, the player's slot.** The career panel's order text names the slot: `<destination> — <first|second|third> line, <left|right> of <neighbour>, <formation>` (screenshot it: the text must read as one sentence inside the order template, on one line); on the map the player's parent brigade or division leaves a gap where the player's command belongs (the AI siblings stand aside, possibly in another row), and the player's own units are never moved or re-formed by Whiskey. `TacticalPlayerSlot` Decision rows (`row`, `column`, `neighbour`, `formation`, `reserved=true`). With no slot found (`reason=no-slot`) the text is today's. A save and reload keeps the order text.
4. **Rollback spot check (optional).** `Tactical Commander Mode = MonitorOnly`: perception lines, `TacticalDecisionMatrix` and operations-ledger rows still appear, but no `producers` / `arbiter-first-fire` line, no `[OrderArbiter]` row and no `TacticalCommandPosture` row (the arbiter and its producers stand down). The same holds after turning `Enabled` off at runtime (ConfigurationManager): the arbiter stands down and every battle write fails closed.

## BC8 — experience and drill

**Branch:** `feat/battle-core-bc8` from `e9d252a` (the integrated battle core). **Goal:** the mod's replacement decisions weigh troop quality where vanilla's own decisions do. Vanilla grows experience and drill untouched by the mod, but the force balance, group stance, odds, sector, withdrawal, charge and campaign offensive thresholds the mod substitutes for vanilla's read headcount only.

**Owned files:** create `Tactical/Battle/TroopQuality.cs` (pure) and `Tactical/Battle/TroopQualityRuntime.cs` (runtime, per-battle prefs reader); modify `BattleCoreContracts.cs` (`UnitRecord.Experience`, `Training`, `LossFraction`, `BreakLossFraction`; `EnemyObservation.Experience`, `Morale`), `BattleSnapshot.cs` (perceived-quality summary), `BattleSnapshotBuilder.cs` (field reads), `BattlePerception.cs` / `BattlePerceptionProbe.cs` (probe columns), `TacticalForceBalanceEvidence.cs`, `TacticalReinforcementOpportunityDoctrine.cs`, `ArmyEvidenceBuilder(.Pure).cs`, `ArmyPostureInputsRuntime.cs`, `TacticalOddsDoctrine.cs`, `TacticalGroupSectorEstimator.cs`, `TacticalBattleCoordinatorRuntime.cs` (`BuildSectorForGroup` only), `Patches/BattleMacroStrategyPatch.cs`, `Patches/TacticalObserverPatch.cs` (sector/odds telemetry inputs only), `TacticalMoralePressure.cs`, `TacticalWithdrawalInputAdapter.cs`, `Producers/WithdrawalIntentProducer(.Plan).cs`, `ChargeDoctrine.cs`, `Producers/ChargeIntentProducer(.Plan).cs`, `Strategic/CoordinatedOperationPackageLedger.cs`, `Patches/CoordinatedOffensiveOperationsPatch.cs`, `Plugin.cs` (one flag, charge description), the test csproj; test file `TroopQualityTests.cs`.

### Vanilla findings (build 14703111)

- **Force balance** (`CalculateSideStatsAndUpdateAITasks` 84570): `forcebalance = totalforceweighted_own / max(1, totalforceweighted_enemy) × (1 + averagemorale_own − averagemorale_enemy) × (1 + averageexperience_own − averageexperience_enemy)` (84862-84863). `averageexperience` is the mean `groupexperience` of the side's top battle-data groups (84654, divided 84836); `averagemorale` the mean unit morale (84758, 84771, divided 84792). `CheckGlobalAIStrategy` compares it with `forcebalancetriggerdefend / attack / assaultflanking / assaultnoflanking`, each divided by `1 + initiative × influenceofcommanderinitiativeonattackdefendtrigger` (6396-6430; battleprefs 0.7).
- **Group strength** (`GetGroupStrength` 6024-6060, `AdjustGroupAIStance` 4238 with `includeexp: true`; also 5962, 6269-6270): own / enemy × `(1 − ei + groupexperience × ei) / (1 − ei + enemyexp × ei)`, `ei = expinfluenceonaidecisions` (battleprefs 0.25; 54714). The enemy term is the closest enemy group's `unitrange.ownexpinrange` (6039-6042). **Vanilla quirk, not ported:** `ownexpinrange` sums `Experience() × morale` without a strength factor (122751) and divides by `ownstrengthinrange` (122894), so for any real force it is ≈ 0 and the divisor is ≈ `1 − ei`. BC8 uses the blend's intended shape on both sides with a perceived enemy experience.
- **Group averages** (`UpdateGroupStrength`): `grouptraining += GetTraining()` (120473), `groupexperience += Experience()` (120508), both divided by the unit count (120671, 120674).
- **`Experience()`** (140114) is a pure getter (W&L player-unit effects 24-26, clamped). **`GetTraining()`** (140537-140549) is not: under W&L it does `training += EffectMultiplier(20, 0, dlcw_isundercommander)` and clamps, on every call (0 for non-player units, a drift for W&L player units until it saturates). Vanilla already calls it every group update (120473). **Decision:** the mod never calls `GetTraining()`; it reads the raw `training` field (which already carries vanilla's drift) and `grouptraining`. This overrides the task's "through GetTraining()" wording.
- **Morale and break** (`UpdateMorale`): a morale drop is amplified by `(2 − Experience()) × overallmoraleinfluenceexp` (128677). `GetUnbearableLossesTrigger` (113359-113404): `num = lerp(unbearablelossesmoraletrigger0, …100, alliance.militaryexperience)`; `num ×= 1 − exp × (1 − influenceexpunbearablelossestrigger)`, `× 1 − GetTraining() × (1 − influencetraining…)`, `× 1 − leadership × (1 − influenceleadership…)`; skirmishers × `unbearablelossestriggerskirmishers`; a non-player alliance × `1 + ((ai_vs_ai ? aicheat_shiftedbonusmoralelosstrigger : 0) + usedcampaignbonusrunning) × aibonus_restoringmorale`; day factor `(lerp(…perdayfactorexp0, …exp100, exp) − 1) × battlepasseddays + 1`; result `min(1, (1 − num) × num4 × num6)`. `Tools.Interpolate` over {0, 1} is an unclamped lerp. A regiment routs from losses when `(ΣLosses − losses[3]) / (that + strength) > trigger` (`LossesAreUnbearable` 139829-139843). `CheckLineFallbacks` (5118-5180) reads morale only.
- **Charge odds** (`UpdateChargeTarget` 122229-122231): `own = strength × (1 + w0) × (1 + Experience() × w1) × (1 + morale × w2) × (1 + (1 − fatigue) × w3)`, the same for `enemyinrangereg[0]`, ratio `own / max(1, enemy)` (× `meleemounted` when mounted: never for infantry) `> microaitriggerforcharge` (122259; battleprefs 3, weights 1 / 0.7 / 1.5 / 0.6).
- **Campaign offensives** (`CheckOffensiveMovements` 14166): units are gathered while `num < enemy × aidominanceforoffensiveoperations × num3` and the offensive is abandoned below it (14256, 14298), `num3 = 1 + (1 − initiative) × commanderinfluenceinitiativeforoperations` of the units' commander in chief (14250, re-read over the gathered list 14281; campaignprefs 0.25: "additional factor needed for offensive operations if commander initiative is 0"). `GetCommanderInChiefOfUnits` (pure) picks the highest `rank × 100 + experience`.

### Fog rule (enemy experience)

Vanilla shows a visible enemy regiment's experience to the player: `UpdateUnitIcon` (121547) returns without drawing unless `isvisible` (121594-121598), `isvisible` is false under `iswithinfog` (127461), and the star block (121832-121852) has no alliance gate: `floor(Experience() × 5)` stars, 0-5, regiments only (a battle group's icon shows 0, 121835-121838). Enemy drill is never shown.

**Decision:** `EnemyObservation.Experience` is the star value the player sees, `TroopQuality.PerceivedExperience(e) = (min(5, max(0, floor(5e))) + 0.5) / 5`, capped at 1 (the bucket midpoint, so the estimate is unbiased; the AI learns no more than the star count). It is written only on Visible probe rows and a Remembered observation keeps the last seen value. Reinforcement columns (groups, unseen) carry none and are left out of every experience mean. Enemy morale, needed by the force balance's `(1 + Δmorale)`, is carried the same way (`EnemyObservation.Morale`, last seen while visible), raw, following the BC6b precedent that reads a visible charge target's morale from its record; vanilla shows the player a 3-band morale alert (121371-121378). Own units are read exactly: `Experience()` and raw `training` for regiments, `groupexperience` / `grouptraining` for groups. The alliance `militaryexperience` proxy is not needed for the enemy (it stays an own-side break-tolerance input).

### Decisions

1. **Snapshot (B).** `UnitRecord.Experience` / `Training` as above, for own and enemy records (enemy records stay perception-only). `PerceptionProbeResult.Experience` / `Morale` columns; `BattlePerception.Update` copies them on visible rows. `BattleSnapshot.PerceivedEnemyQuality` (a `SideQuality`: `Morale`, `Experience`, `Known`; `TroopQuality.PerceivedQuality`, mean over observations, visible weight 1, remembered weight `Confidence01`, reinforcement rows excluded; `Known` false with none). Own means: `TroopQuality.OwnQuality` over alive, active, unrouted combat regiments (the `OwnAverages` filter). Zero allocation preserved (assembler test).
2. **Force balance (C).** `TacticalForceBalanceEvidence` gets own / enemy mean morale and experience; `CurrentRatio = own / enemy × (1 + ΔM) × (1 + ΔE)` (vanilla 84862 shape, sanitized ≥ 0; the no-enemy cases keep their 10 / 1 values). `TacticalReinforcementOpportunityDoctrine.EffectiveAttackThreshold` divides by `1 + initiative × influenceofcommanderinitiativeonattackdefendtrigger` after the parity cap, as vanilla divides its triggers (the evidence already carried the live initiative but `Decide` never read it). With the shipped 0.7 a mid-initiative commander's Meeting/Attacker threshold falls from parity to 0.74 (vanilla's own attack trigger is 0.80 / 1.35 ≈ 0.59); a Defender never attacks. #44's macro mapping reads the same quality-weighted ratio.
3. **Group stance / odds / sector (D).** `TacticalGroupContactInput` takes an own and an enemy blend; `BuildSector` scales own strength by the own blend and every enemy strength term by the enemy blend. Each blend is vanilla's GetGroupStrength product (6052-6057, `includemorale` and `includeexp`): `(1 − mi + morale × mi) × (1 − ei + experience × ei)` (`TroopQuality.AiDecisionBlend`; mi / ei = `moraleinfluenceonaidecisions` / `expinfluenceonaidecisions`, 54712 / 54714, both 0.25). Own = the group's `groupmorale` (120158 / 120670; `UnitRecord.GroupMorale`) and `groupexperience`; enemy = the closest visible observation's perceived morale and stars (vanilla's closest enemy group, 6039; vanilla's `ownmoraleinrange` is Σ morale × strength / `ownstrengthinrange` (122750 / 122892), whose denominator also adds a group's own `groupstrengthdirect` (122886-122889), so it is not a plain strength-weighted mean; the perceived morale is the blend's intended shape, as for experience), else the side's perceived means (visible plus confidence-weighted remembered), else neutral (1). Call sites: the coordinator's `BuildSectorForGroup` (feeds the GroupStance producer), #44's legacy `BuildSectorAssessments`, the observer's telemetry `BuildPerceivedSector`. `TacticalOddsInput` takes the same two blends for its global odds (own side mean vs perceived mean). `ei` / `mi` are read once per battle (`TroopQualityRuntime`, BattleScope), and so is the rollback flag: a runtime toggle applies from the next battle (the campaign scaling of decision 6 reads the flag on each call).
4. **Withdrawal (E).** The snapshot carries each own regiment's `LossFraction` (LossesAreUnbearable 139829-139843: the smaller of L / (L + strength) and L / (L + strength + the detached regiment's strength), since vanilla routs only when both exceed the trigger, 139832 / 139834-139839) and `BreakLossFraction` = `TroopQuality.BreakLossFraction` (vanilla 113363-113400 from explicit inputs: alliance `militaryexperience`, `Experience()`, raw `training`, commander `GetCommanderLeadership()`, the prefs, battle day). `TacticalMoralePressure`: a unit under fire whose loss fraction has reached `NearBreakShare` (0.8) of its own break point is a `WithdrawalCandidate` (B8 ladder → RearGuard → vanilla's fallback shape), so a veteran, drilled, well-led regiment holds longer than a green one at the same losses. Vanilla's own rout stays authoritative.
5. **Charge (E).** `ChargeInput` gets own/target strength, experience and fatigue and vanilla's weights and trigger (read once per battle); `ChargeDoctrine.ConditionsHold` also requires `TroopQuality.VanillaChargeOdds(…) > microaitriggerforcharge`. The target's experience is its perceived (star) value, its strength/morale/fatigue its visible record (BC6b precedent). Vanilla's check after arming stays the final word; the pre-filter only stops arm → refusal cycles, each of which costs the 1800 battle-s back-off. An already armed regiment is not reverted by the odds (vanilla's check decides it).
6. **Campaign (F).** `CoordinatedOperationOptions.ScaledByCommanderInitiative(initiative, influence)` multiplies `RequiredAttackRatio` and `RequiredReinforceRatio` by `1 + (1 − initiative) × influence` (14250/14281 shape). Applied only on #38's vanilla-offensive replacement (`CoordinatedOffensiveOperationsPatch.BuildAllowedSet`), with the live initiative of the lead's commander: vanilla's first read (14250) is over the lead's own group, whose commander in chief is the lead's commander; the re-read over the gathered list (14281) is not reproduced. Operational probes keep their CIC-personality options (`StrategicCoordinator` `FromDirector`).
7. **Rollback.** One flag, `Tactical` / `Enable Experience And Drill AI Weighting` (default true). Off: neutral prefs (`ei` 0, initiative influences 0, no break input, no charge-odds pre-filter) and equal side qualities, i.e. the BC7 headcount behaviour. `TroopQualityRuntime` is the only reader.

### Smoke watch (added to the BC7 checklist, Session A items 2.12-2.14)

See `### BC7 smoke checklist`, items 2.12 (army attack/hold), 2.13 (withdrawal and charge), 2.14 (campaign offensives).

### Tests (`TroopQualityTests`, red first)

`PerceivedExperienceIsTheStarMidpoint`, `ProbeCarriesExperienceOnlyWhenVisible`, `RememberedKeepsLastSeenExperience`, `PerceivedQualityWeightsRememberedByConfidence`, `ReinforcementRowsHaveNoQuality`, `SnapshotQualityAllocatesNothingAfterWarmup`, `ForceBalanceMatchesVanillaShape` (84862 at fixed points), `VeteranOutnumberedAttacks` / `GreenEarlyWarHolds` (doctrine), `AttackThresholdDividedByInitiative` (0.80 / 1.35 pin), `DefenderStillNeverAttacks`, `AiDecisionBlendMatchesVanilla` (6057), `SectorScalesOwnAndEnemyByBlend`, `OddsUseExperienceBlends`, `BreakLossFractionMatchesVanilla` (113363-113400), `NearBreakUnderFireWithdraws` / `VeteranHoldsAtSameLosses`, `ChargeOddsMatchVanilla` (122229-122231), `ChargeRefusedBelowVanillaOdds`, `OffensiveRatioScaledByInitiative` (×1.25 at 0, ×1 at 1), `FlagOffIsHeadcountOnly`.

PROVE-MODE: harness for every pure rule above (`TroopQualityTests` plus the touched doctrine tests; red first, then mutation checks on the force-balance factor, the blend, the star quantization, the break formula and the initiative scaling). Runtime proof is in-game smoke (items added to the BC7 smoke checklist, Session A items 2.12-2.14): the snapshot's quality reads, the `TacticalReinforcementOpportunity` ratio moving with experience, charges only where vanilla's odds pass, and `[CoordinatedOps]` packages against the initiative-scaled ratio. No new patch, so no new first-fire id.

**As built (BC8):**
- Commits: design `1d8140c`; B+C `e651f78` (snapshot experience, perceived enemy quality, force balance, initiative division, flag); D `714dd51` (sector / odds blends at the coordinator, #44 and the observer); E `13f6372` (break tolerance, charge odds pre-filter); F `53f2e51` (coordinated offensives).
- `TacticalForceBalanceEvidence.EffectiveEnemyStrength` (enemy / quality factor, floored at `MinQualityFactor` 0.01) replaces the raw enemy in `CurrentRatio`, both parity-hours tests and the doctrine's parity-through-window test, so a veteran army that is slightly outnumbered in men but not in own-equivalent men can attack. The evidence's `CommanderInitiative01` (carried since T-series, never read by `Decide`) is now the divisor's input.
- The initiative division takes effect at AttackNow only where the ratio also clears the doctrine's parity tests (`enemyNotGrowing` or own arrivals restoring parity inside the attack window); a lower threshold alone does not make an outnumbered army attack.
- **Initiative per ladder branch, matched to vanilla (review follow-up).** `CheckGlobalAIStrategy` divides the defend trigger (6400, `forcebalance < forcebalancetriggerdefend / (1 + initiative × 0.7)`), the attack trigger (6403) and both assault triggers (6407, 6421); it does not divide the retreat trigger `GetScreeningTrigger()` = `forcebalancetriggerattack × 0.5` (4216, read at 6414 and by AdjustGroupAIStance 4229). Shipped battleprefs set defend = attack = 0.80, so vanilla has one divided boundary between defending and attacking. The mod's ladder follows that: AttackNow, WaitAndConsolidate and DelayingDefense compare against the divided `attackThreshold` (the attack/defend boundary); WithdrawalToFightLater (`ComputeWithdrawalRatioThreshold`, 0.40 at mid aggression = vanilla's 0.80 × 0.5) and the prolonged-delay escalation's 0.50 stay undivided, as vanilla's retreat; DefensiveHold is the fall-through with no macro opinion. Consequence: a Meeting/Attacker force balance in [divided threshold, 1) (≈ [0.74, 1) at mid initiative) with no near own arrivals is above vanilla's divided defend boundary, so it is no longer DelayingDefense; it reads DefensiveHold and vanilla's own macro AI decides there (it attacks, 6403-6406, as it would without the mod). A Defender shifts the same way in ≈ [0.98, 1.32). Tests: `LadderAttackBoundaryIsDividedLikeVanillaAttack`, `LadderWaitBandIsBelowTheDividedThreshold`, `LadderDelayingDefenseIsBelowTheDividedThreshold`, `LadderWithdrawalIsUndividedLikeVanillaRetreat`, `LadderProlongedEscalationIsUndivided`, `LadderDefensiveHoldAboveTheDividedThreshold` (mutation: dividing the withdrawal threshold turned the withdrawal test red).
- Leadership cache refreshes are staggered by commander id (window × 1 to 1.875) so the per-regiment `GetCommanderLeadership` walks do not all land in one `tactical.snapshot.build`.
- `UnitRecord`: `Experience` / `Training` for every record (enemy records stay perception-only); `LossFraction` / `BreakLossFraction` for own infantry/cavalry regiments with the weighting on (the withdrawal doctrine's candidates, `PlanTacticalFallback`), 0 otherwise. Commander leadership is cached per commander for `TroopQualityRuntime.LeadershipRefreshHours` (0.25 doctrine h): `GetCommanderLeadership` walks the parent command (61043-61080) and would cost a transform walk per regiment per tick.
- `EnemyObservation.Morale` is raw (BC6b precedent); `Experience` is the star midpoint. Own quality is the unweighted mean over alive, active, unrouted combat regiments (`TroopQuality.OwnQuality`), the perceived one over observations (visible 1, remembered by confidence).
- Group stance and odds weigh morale as well as experience (review follow-up): `UnitRecord.GroupMorale` (groups only; a group record's `Morale` stays its own field, which posture and the division play read), `TroopQualityPrefs.MoraleInfluence`, `SectorBlends` / `SideBlends` multiply the two blends (tests `SectorBlendsWeighGroupMorale`, `SideBlendsWeighMorale`; mutation: dropping the own morale blend turned the sector test red).
- Telemetry: `TacticalReinforcementOpportunity` gains `qualityFactor` (and `|quality=` in its signature); `CoordinatedOps` package rows gain `requiredAttackRatio`; the decision matrix's known force balance is vanilla-shaped.
- Verify landing items (round 1, verdict pass): tests pin each surviving plant — `ProducerOddsReadTheTargetsStars` (charge odds use the observation's stars, not the record's true or the regiment's own experience; M24 / M12), `ParityHoursUseOwnEquivalentMen` (M16), `AttackWindowParityUsesOwnEquivalentMen` (M23), `SectorLineContactEnemyIsBlended` (M19); each plant re-applied turned its test red (exit 134). Loss fraction now includes vanilla's second check with the detached regiment's strength (139834-139839, `detachedregiment` 111134, `GetComponent` only when set); the detached case in `LossFractionMatchesVanilla` turned red without it.
- Harness: 1915 → 1947 PASS on the BC8 base (32 new: 30 `TroopQualityTests`, `ChargeDoctrineTests.ProducerArmsOnlyWhenVanillaOddsPass`, `ReserveMigrationTests.WithdrawalNearBreakPointFallsBack`); after the main merge (1917 on main) and the review follow-ups (8 more `TroopQualityTests`: two morale, six ladder) 1957 PASS; after the verify landing items 1961 PASS. Mutation evidence (each exit 134): no break input in the withdrawal plan, no arming pre-filter, no drill term in the break formula.

## BC9 — reconnaissance and probing

**Branch:** `feat/battle-recon` from `a5cce64` (battle core BC0-BC8 merged). **Goal:** the fog-honest AI (BC2 perception, the contact ledger's intel confidence, the 0.35 action gate) must go and look. Vanilla's battle AI never scouts because it never needs to; with Whiskey's AI fog-honest, a side with nothing actively looking stalls in Hold below the gate or advances blind on scripted objectives. BC9 adds cavalry reconnaissance, reconnaissance in force in the Probe phase, a farther skirmisher screen, and folds in the half-built recon pieces already on `main` (coordinator survey, 2026-10-15).

### Vanilla and W&L model (build 14703111)

- **Omniscient AI, so no scouting.** `forcebalance` is true weighted strength of both sides (`CalculateSideStatsAndUpdateAITasks` 84641-84651, 84862-84863); `GetClosestEnemyUnit` (123760) and `closestenemyunitfar*` read every enemy (`UpdateUnitRangeFast` 122658-122663); `CheckEnemyContact` (117155-117383) stops moves on the true closest enemy; nothing reads the AI-side fog grid `fows[1]` (`docs/vanilla-vision-and-movement.md`). Vanilla has no recon task, role or order.
- **Skirmisher companies (type 3) are accidental scouts.** `CheckSkirmishing` (5343-5406, per AI group from the micro AI) detaches row-0 infantry (`GetRow() == 0`) as companies only while the regiment stands (`regimentpaths <= 0`), the group is in screen (1) or defend (2) or its path is interrupted, morale ≥ `aiminmoraletodeployskirmishers`, out of cover, and no enemy is inside the regiment's fire range (5379-5390). It reattaches them when the group goes to assault, the company's morale drops, it is in melee, the army retreats (macroai 3), the parent moves in screen/defend, or an enemy is within `aimindistancereattachskirmishers` (5354-5363). So a vanilla AI skirmisher screen exists only in front of a **stationary** screen/defend line.
- **`DetachCompany`** (90430-90515) instantiates a copy of the regiment, sets `parentregiment` to the source regiment, parents the copy to the source's transform parent (the brigade) and copies `dlcw_isundercommander` (90477). `DetachUnits` ends with `UpdateUnitList` (90239), so the company is in `completeunitlist`: **detached companies are already own records in the battle snapshot**, with the D5 command parent = the brigade (the coordinator's "excluded from the snapshot" refers to the direct-child probe filter, `BattleSnapshotAssembler.IsDirectChild`, which keeps them out of the command tree's direct children, as vanilla's `GetAttachedUnitsReg(excludeskirmishers)` 119840-119880 does). What the snapshot lacked: the source regiment and a company flag.
- **Skirmisher distance.** A type-3 company links to its parent regiment (113061, re-linked by `CheckNewTargetsOfSkirmishers` 128846-128920 when nothing is in its auto-target range). `GetUnitLinkData` (124208-124390) puts a front link (linkedposition 0) at `unitlinkdistance` (10, unitprefs 1069) + `skirmisherdistance` (20, unitprefs 864) = **30 m** ahead of the parent regiment (the brief's "20 m" is the skirmisher term alone), and `UpdateUnitLink` (124396-124500) re-paths the company there on every `updatecyclelinkingbrigades` cycle while no enemy is inside its fire range (`GetClosestEnemyUnit(firerange) == null`, 124467). `SetWaypoint` clears the link of a type 3/4 unit (91514-91516), so moving a company would only last until vanilla re-links it.
- **Cavalry scout companies (type 4) cannot be steered.** `CheckSkirmishing`'s third block detaches them only in screen stance while the cavalry regiment moves (5395-5405; `DetachmentPossible` 7: not dismounted, 90414). `CheckScoutMovingTargets` (124616-124660, every Regiment update) re-paths a scout to its parent's path target whenever it is more than `distanceformovingtargetsscouts` (5 m, unitprefs 1014) off and stops it when the parent has no path. Decision: **the recon vehicle is a whole cavalry regiment** (unittyp 1); a cavalry regiment moving in a screen-stance brigade also throws out vanilla's own 450 m-sight scout company (maximumlineofsight[4], unitprefs 205).
- **Cavalry groups are linked.** `SeparateUnitsToGroups` (7231-7261) moves cavalry reserves (oob symbol 1) into each objective chain's `screeninggroups` when that chain has infantry (`AreInfantryUnitsInGroup`, 7242); `LinkReservesToLineGroup` (6729-6742) links every screening group behind the centre (linkedposition 2) and `AssignReserves` (7102-7119) links the most suitable one to each unanchored flank (linkedposition 3/1). `UpdateUnitLink` re-lays a linked **group** with `SetGroupFormation` whenever its last waypoint is farther than the recommended distance from the link target, so a group-level recon move is undone at the next link cycle. A regiment move is undone only when its group is re-laid (`SetGroupFormation` 92076-92108), i.e. when vanilla or Whiskey moves or re-forms the group.
- **Contact.** `CheckEnemyContact` stops a moving regiment's AI group within `distancetoenemyforinterruption` (100 m, unitprefs 989) of the closest enemy, **except in screen stance** (`ai_stance != 1`, 117253): a screen-stance unit walks on into contact. The interruption is cancelled beyond `distancetoenemytocancelinterruption` (150 m, unitprefs 991). `CheckFallbackDueToEnemyClose` (128924-129013) pulls a regiment in screen stance (or with combat behaviour 4, evade) back when an enemy is within `minimumdistancetonextenemyunittofallback` (25 m): vanilla's own on-contact give-way for screens. `AdjustCavalryBehavior` (5036-5076) charges weak enemies within `maxdistancecavalryattack` (90 m) only outside screen/defend stance, and mounts a moving dismounted cavalry regiment (5072-5075).
- **Formations.** `AdjustGroupFormations` (5875-5934) gives a settled screen-stance group formation 0 (line; group formation names "Line, Double Line, Successive Lines, Attack Column, March Column", 5883), only when not moving. `CheckFormationChange` (127625-127682) switches a regiment into march column (formation 3) itself for a move longer than `mindistanceformarchcolumn` with no enemy within the interruption distance, and back near the destination. Decision: recon and probe moves carry **no formation intent**; vanilla columns the regiment, and the probe group keeps `CommandFormationCorrection.TargetFormationForTask(Probe)` (its current formation unless march column, then line).
- **Regiment formation 4 is the square** (`firearctype = 2` for `formation == 4`, 118174; four all-round fire arcs, 118262-118270; tooltips_battle 107 "Square — immobile formation to stop a cavalry charge").
- **Unit types.** unitprefs: 0 infantry, 1 cavalry, 2 artillery, 3 skirmishers, 4 scouts, 5 courier (unitprefs 4-225). The mod named 4 `Officer` and 5 `Excluded`; BC9 renames them `Scout` and `Courier` (cavalry follow's "target is an officer" matched scout companies).
- **Weakness screen and blind flanks.** `AdjustGroupAIStance` puts groups in screen only as a weakness fallback (4216, 4221-4278); `CheckFlankMoves` (6247-6311) and `FlankMove` (116929) march to flank positions from true positions, never after a look.
- **W&L.** `PerformAIActionDLCWL` (5101-5116): under the career vanilla's per-unit AI actions (skirmisher detach/reattach, cavalry behaviour, perks) skip a unit under the player's command (`dlcw_isundercommander`) unless the player's own group has an AI stance (≥ 1) and the unit belongs to that AI group (5107-5109), so a W&L player in stance mode gets vanilla's auto-skirmishers. A detached company copies its regiment's `dlcw_isundercommander` (90477), so `PlayerOwnership` marks it `PlayerOwned` like its regiment. `ai_vs_ai` (forced by W&L) runs the player's alliance's AIBattle, so both sides scout; the player's own command never takes part (guard, below).
- **Whiskey on `main` before BC9 (code survey).** `CommandNodeRole.Probe` was never assigned (`CommandNodeOperationsRuntime.MapRole` has no Probe case). The operation director entered Scouting only on `recon-contact` (needs EnemyStrength > 0 and confidence ≥ 0.25, `TacticalDecisionDoctrine.IsReconnaissanceContact`); with no contact at all `Odds(own, 0)` ≥ 1.35 committed the operation (`TacticalOperationDirector.SelectOperation`, "selected"). `BattlePhase.Probe` changed no unit task (only `ArmyReplanTriggers` read it). The GT recon doctrine turned attack/recon tasks without a visible enemy into Scout (any cavalry anywhere in the subtree) or Probe and aimed them at an objective stand-off (`TacticalBattleLinePlanner`, `TacticalNavMeshPlanner`, `PostureTargets.TryObjectiveApproach`), never at unwatched ground. Cavalry follow passed constants for `targetHidden`, `targetInSquare`, `leaderAllowsCharge`, `atFollowLocation` (the last unread by the doctrine); `RequestScoutLocation` had no generator. Fire control passed `mounted: false`, so the cavalry screen-evade branch was dead. The facing pulse's `RecentFire` / `Objective` / `Entry` threat sources were never produced.

### Decisions

1. **Snapshot (pure contract, additive).** `UnitFlags.DetachedCompany` (type 3/4 with a `parentregiment`), `UnitFlags.Mounted` (`mounted >= 1`), `UnitFlags.Linked` (`unitlinkedto != null`); `UnitRecord.ParentRegimentId` (the company's source regiment, 0 otherwise). Field reads in `BattleSnapshotBuilder.Capture`. Own-regiment loops are audited so companies do not newly qualify: the recon candidate filter and the skirmisher push read the flag; withdrawal (`CheckLineFallbacks` 5137 filters by type, the doctrine keeps vanilla's unit filter), fire control (skirmishers are fire-control units as shipped) and charge (infantry type 0 only) are unchanged.
2. **Ledger read API.** `TacticalEnemyContactLedger` keeps its 25 m lateral cells and gains (a) per-entry-point watch stamps (pooled, keyed by the entry point's instance id, positions with them), fed by `ArmyEvidenceBuilder.BuildEnemyVisibleState` where it already computes the watched set (its entry list is pooled, no per-build `List`), and (b) `TryStalestBand` (the current coverage band with the lowest recency, its lateral centre in the ledger's world-anchored frame and the frame heading). The ledger resets its band cells when the frame turns more than 1 degree (`ObserveCoverage`); the entry-point stamps are keyed by instance id and survive the turn (`ObserveEntryPoints`). Recon starts are bounded per hour and per target, so a band reset alone never floods them. `ArmyEvidenceBuilder.ContactLedger(alliance)` becomes an internal read accessor.
3. **Recon planner (pure, `Tactical/Battle/BattleRecon.cs`) is the single owner of "where to look".** Per side, per arbiter tick, on the snapshot and the ledger picture:
   - *Trigger:* no mission running, fewer than `MaxMissionStartsPerHour` (4) starts in the last doctrine hour, and either intel confidence below `TacticalReinforcementOpportunityDoctrine.MinIntelConfidenceForAction` (0.35), a stale enemy entry point, or a stale band.
   - *Target, in order:* the nearest enemy-side entry point not watched for `StaleHours` (1 h; recency 0.5 on the ledger's 2 h scale) or never; then the stalest coverage band below recency 0.5; then, below the gate, straight ahead along the snapshot front axis with no contact ever, or the nearest remembered (no longer visible) enemy after contact (review fix: confidence also falls when contact itself grows old, `contactRecency`, with every band observed and every entry point watched). A target within `TargetRepeatMeters` (100 m) of one picked in the last hour is skipped. The stand point: an entry point is watched by a unit within its line of sight (`MarkVisibleEntryPoints`, maximumlineofsight), so the scout stands `ObservationStandOff` = `distancetoenemytocancelinterruption` (150 m, unitprefs 991; vanilla's own "out of contact" distance, inside cavalry sight 250) short of it, on the line from the scout; a band is observed when an own combat unit stands in it, so the scout goes to the band's lateral centre at the own line's mean forward depth; the forward point is the own line's depth plus `2 × maximumlineofsight[cavalry]` (500 m).
   - *Scout:* an own cavalry regiment (unittyp 1, not a company), alive, active, mounted or not (vanilla mounts a moving cavalry regiment, 5070-5073), standing (no path, no queued order), not routed / marked for rout / on retreat / in melee / garrisoned / permanently detached, not `PlayerOwned`, not `Feud`, morale ≥ `aiminmoraletodeployskirmishers` (vanilla's own bar for sending men out, 5381/5400), no visible enemy within `ObservationStandOff`. Its command parent must be an AI-controlled group that is not moving, has no queued order, is not in assault stance, and did not get a posture move this tick (`PostureTick.Current(s).Groups[parent].Submitted`): the executor's move shield (`BuildMoveShield`) protects only higher-priority claims, so a Support recon move would be re-pathed by its brigade's Executor move — candidate selection is the protection. The closest qualifying regiment to the stand point wins.
   - *Safety:* never toward a visible superior enemy — the stand point is rejected when a visible enemy whose strength is at least the scout's lies within `ObservationStandOff` of it or within `CorridorMeters` (= the 100 m interruption distance) of the straight path.
   - *Mission (state on the doctrine clock, BattleScope store, advanced only on executor outcomes, BC6b pattern):* `Outbound` — a Move intent (Support, `IntentSource.Recon`, task Scout, by courier `UseOrderDelay` as vanilla AI moves go) re-submitted each tick (the executor dedupes it); confirmed only when `TryGetLastOutcome(scout, Move)` is Issued by Recon at or after the submission, else the mission ends (fail closed). `Observe` on arrival (within `OrderArbiter.DedupeMeters` of the stand point or standing after a confirmed move) or on first sight of an enemy within `ObservationStandOff` (a `Stop` intent then); dwell `ObserveDwellHours` (0.05 h) so the heavy evidence pass stamps the ground. `Return` — a Move to the scout's layout slot in its brigade (`LeafSlotTarget`, the shipped #61 slot rule), else to its brigade's position; done within 60 m. Contact: a visible enemy at least as strong within the interruption distance ends the outbound or observe leg early into `Return`; fire makes it the Withdrawal producer's (Safety beats Support), whose move ends the mission (`Recalled`). A scout whose last waypoint vanilla moved off ours (a group re-lay) is `Recalled`. A mission ends after `MaxMissionHours` (0.5 h) whatever its leg.
   - *Look points for Scout/Probe tasks:* `BattleRecon.LookPoint(s, group)` = the group's position advanced `ProbeAdvanceMeters` = maximumlineofsight[infantry] (200 m, unitprefs 29: one sight depth further) along the snapshot front axis, rejected toward a visible superior enemy like the scout's. `PostureTargets.TryResolve` takes the target of a Scout or Probe task from it before the line-planner/objective fallback (the GT recon "by bounds" advance, now a bounded one); with the BC9 flag off the shipped objective stand-off applies.
4. **Reconnaissance in force (probe nomination).** In `BattlePhase.Probe`, for a side that is not the battle's Defender (`ArmyOrchestrator.CurrentBattleStance`, decision 12), the planner nominates per front band (snapshot front axis, 5 bands) with no visible enemy one AI-pool group: a brigade when the band has one, else the smallest group; not moving, no queued order, not linked (`UnitFlags.Linked`: vanilla's own link would re-lay it), not routed, no player-owned unit in its subtree, not artillery-only, not in assault. At most one start per band per doctrine hour (`MaxProbeStartsPerBandPerHour` 1). A nomination ends on visible contact (any visible enemy within maximumlineofsight[infantry] of the group: the recon producer then submits one `Stop` at Support for it, because a screen-stance group does not stop itself, 117253), on arrival, when the side leaves the Probe phase, or after `MaxProbeHours` (0.25 h); what follows the end is decision 13. It is consumed by `PosturePipeline` as a task rewrite — role `CommandNodeRole.Probe`, task `Probe`, target the nomination's look point — so the posture producer issues it at Executor priority with the mapper's screen stance (`TacticalCommandTaskToStanceMapper`: Probe → 1). The Probe role is thus assigned by the probe rewrite, not by `CommandNodeOperationsRuntime.MapRole` (the allocator's `DirectChildRole` has no Probe).
5. **Director: no contact means scouting.** `TacticalOperationDirector.SelectOperation`: a primary objective with no enemy strength (`NoUsableContact` / zero-enemy `UnreliableContact`) selects `Scouting` ("no-contact-scout"), never `Committed` on `Odds(own, 0)`. `CommandDoctrineAssignment.ResolveTask` gives the recon tasks (`ReconnaissanceTaskFor`) in Scouting also without contact, except for `DefensiveNetwork` / `DelayAndFallback` shapes (a defender is not sent forward). Consequence: `OperationMacroAi(Scouting) = -1` leaves the side's macro opinion to vanilla during the search, as it did for `recon-contact`. Existing director tests that asserted the commit on zero enemy are updated with this reason.
6. **GT recon doctrine.** The cavalry branch (any cavalry in the subtree → group Scout) is deleted: regiment scouting is the recon producer's. Attack tasks without a visible enemy become Probe (Screen for screening roles), aimed by the look point. For a Defender the stance guard (decision 12) runs after this, with or without a visible enemy: Probe → Screen, Attack/Support/Fix → HoldObjective.
7. **Cavalry follow and fire control.** `targetInSquare` = the visible target's own record in regiment formation 4 (118174; BC6b precedent for reading a visible target's record); `targetIsOfficer` = a command group or a courier (unittyp > 13 or 5), not a scout company; `targetHidden` (structurally false on a closest-visible target), `leaderAllowsCharge` (constant true) and `atFollowLocation` (unread) are deleted from the input. Fire control passes the snapshot's `Mounted`, so the cavalry screen-evade branch (behaviour 4, `CheckFallbackDueToEnemyClose`'s give-way, 128955) is live.
8. **Facing.** A Screen / Probe / GuardFlank brigade with no visible threat faces the stalest unwatched enemy entry point (`TacticalFacingThreatSource.Entry`, never urgent); `RecentFire` and `Objective` are deleted (no producer). The facing pulse submits it through `PostureIntentPlan.SubmitBrigadePulse`, whose group-facing gate `CommandFormationCorrection.ShouldFaceThreat(task, source)` accepts the Entry source for Screen and Probe (GuardFlank already faced threats). Regiments: on a clean subtree the group facing turns them; under a brigade with a detached or player-owned descendant each regiment with nothing visible takes the brigade's Entry bearing (the doctrine does not apply the fire-range gate to an entry point). Every other task (HoldObjective, ReserveWait, Consolidate, FixEnemy, Delay, HoldChoke, FallBackToLine) is refused the Entry source (`no-threat-bearing`) and keeps its front-axis facing with no new write, as before BC9. The Entry source also opens the pulse's formation channel for a Screen/Probe brigade, whose target (`TargetFormationForTask`: a march column goes to line, else the current formation) is the posture pipeline's own for these tasks.
9. **Skirmisher screen distance (#80 `SkirmisherScreenDistancePatch`, Postfix on `Regiment.GetUnitLinkData`).** Vanilla re-links companies every cycle (124467), so Whiskey steers that input instead of fighting it with moves: for an AI type-3 company on a front link whose command group is in screen stance (or attack stance while moving), with an empty own range list (`FirstEnemyInRangeId == 0`), not in melee and no visible enemy within `distancetoenemytocancelinterruption` + the push, the returned link target is moved forward along the link rotation to `distancetoenemyforinterruption` (100 m, unitprefs 989) ahead of the parent: the distance at which vanilla stops a moving line on contact, so the skirmish line meets the enemy first and, with its 250 m sight (unitprefs 161), sees 350 m past the line (the line's own 200 m sight, unitprefs 29). The result's distance fields are recomputed from the new point exactly as `GetUnitLinkData` computes them (124370-124386), so `UpdateUnitLink`'s gates see a consistent record. On contact the condition fails and vanilla's 30 m returns by itself. Result override of vanilla's own input (no Prefix-skip, no write call, no persisted field: `linkedoffset` is saved with the unit, 73773, so it is not used); guarded by `TacticalWriteGuard.CanWrite(Regiment)` (W&L player companies untouched), Commander Mode Active, the BC9 flag.
10. **Producer and priority.** `Recon` (`ReconIntentProducer`, Support) joins `BattleProducerRegistry.Order` after `DivisionPlay` (it reads the posture producer's per-group record of this tick) and before `ReserveRelease`: 12 producers. Support is right: recon yields to every doctrine move of the same unit; its protection is the candidate filter above. `IntentSource.Recon` is appended (no renumbering).
11. **Rollback.** One flag, `Tactical` / `Enable Battle Reconnaissance` (default true): the recon producer, the probe nomination, the probe hold, the look points, the facing Entry source and `SkirmisherScreenDistancePatch`. Off = the shipped objective stand-off for Scout/Probe and no recon moves. The director's no-contact rule and its Defender rule, the GT recon cavalry-branch deletion and Defender screen, and the cavalry-follow / fire-control input fixes are bug fixes and are not behind the flag.
12. **Defender stance (integrator decisions, review rounds 1 and 2).** A Defender side (vanilla `GetBattleType` 2) never gets reconnaissance in force, a Probe task or an attack-stance task (`AttackObjective`, `SupportAttack`, `FixEnemy`: attack stance 3 in `TacticalCommandTaskToStanceMapper`), as AGENTS.md's "a Defender never gets attack doctrine"; it still screens and scouts with the recon producer's cavalry (information-gathering, not an attack). One stance: `ArmyOrchestrator.CurrentBattleStance`, read by `ArmyEvidenceBuilder.ReadBattleStance` (GetBattleType without the readiness tie-break) and refreshed by `ArmyPostureInputsRuntime.Refresh` on every coordinator tick before its `Enable Tactical Reinforcement Opportunity Doctrine` gate (`RefreshPostureInputs` runs before `DriveOperationsLedger`, so the ledger sees this tick's stance). The rule holds on every path: (a) the director (`TacticalOperationDirectorInput.WithStance`, from `StoreDoctrineForCommandStates`) passes every decision through `ForDefender`: once an objective is selected, any shape but `DefensiveNetwork` / `DelayAndFallback` becomes `DefensiveNetwork` and a `Committed` / `Exploiting` phase becomes `Forming` (reasons `defender-screen` in Scouting, `defender-hold` otherwise), so no new selection, earlier commit, downgrade, commit window or hysteresis leaves a Defender in an offensive shape; (b) `TacticalDecisionDoctrine.TaskForStance` guards every task assignment: where node tasks are planned (`CommandNodeOperationsRuntime.Build` / `TryBuildSingle`, with the army stance: so `army.CurrentCommandOperations` and every reader of it, #45's ledger branch, the `TacticalCommandAssignment` rows, the posture fallback state and the W&L player-order adapter, see the guarded task), `CommandDoctrineAssignment.Build` (any shape, any phase), the GT recon doctrine (with or without a visible enemy), the posture pipeline after all its rewrites (GT recon, cavalry follow, local emergency, opportunity override; the division play runs through the same pipeline) and the leaf cascade; Probe → Screen, Attack/Support/Fix → HoldObjective, everything else unchanged; (c) the probe nomination reads the same stance (decision 4). The posture pipeline and leaf-cascade guard is the pure `PostureStanceGuard` (`Apply` / `Task`, in the harness) and source-pinned tests fail if either call or its place after the last rewrite is removed. (d) Stance: #45's ledger branch maps the guarded task with `MapForGroupStanceScorer(task, weakPoint, stance)`, which caps a Defender at defend (2), the visible-weak-point escalation of a hold included: Whiskey never issues attack stance for a Defender from a ledger task; vanilla's own stance cascade may still counterattack on its own. (e) Integrator decision: the BC6 charge doctrine stays as it is for a Defender, since it only arms charges for groups vanilla itself escalated to assault (vanilla's decision, allowed as vanilla allows it). (f) Integrator decision: #45's default sector scorer (`TacticalDoctrineScorer.DecideGroupStance`, for groups no ledger state resolves) takes `sideDefending` (`TacticalGroupStanceDecisionInput`, from the side's `CurrentBattleStance`; the decision-matrix telemetry passes the same) and caps its two escalations at defend (2) for a Defender: `attack-weak-point` becomes `defender-weak-point-hold`, `defensive-counterstroke` becomes `defender-counterstroke-hold`. With (d), Whiskey never issues attack stance for a Defender; vanilla's own stance cascade may still counterattack. One stance reader: the division play (`PlayIntentProducer`) reads `CurrentBattleStance` too, and `ArmyPostureInputsRuntime._defending` / `SideDefending` (the readiness tie-break) are deleted.
13. **After a probe (integrator decision).** A probe that ends with a visible enemy at least as strong within one infantry sight depth (strength ≥ the group's, as the look point's safety) is over: the group falls back to its ledger task, as before. Any other end (weaker contact, arrival, `MaxProbeHours`) leaves a hold: the group holds the ground it gained (its position when the probe ended; on arrival the probe point) until the ledger reassigns it. `ReconMemory` keeps the hold in a grow-only store with one slot per holding group (a new hold never takes another group's slot; at most one hold per own group, so it stops growing at the roster) (position, the probe's advance direction and the posture task the group had when nominated, `ReconPicture.PostureTask` from `PostureTick`'s group record: the posture producer runs before Recon); the posture pipeline asks `ReconIntentProducer.TryHold` with its final task and drops a posture move whose target lies more than the arbiter's dedupe radius (60 m) behind the held point along the advance (`BattleRecon.HoldBlocks`): no intent, the group stands (a dropped move also drops that tick's stance intent, which the posture executor sends with the move, so a held group usually keeps the probe's screen stance until a posture decision changes stance on its own); forward and along-the-line targets go (a Probe/Scout task keeps advancing by bounds), and a break-off, fallback or recovery keeps its own target. No direct write: the hold only withholds the posture producer's own move intents. The hold ends when the group's posture task differs from the nomination's (the ledger reassigned it, or an emergency/opportunity rewrite), the group is gone, a visible enemy at least as strong comes within one infantry sight depth, a new probe nominates the group, or the flag is turned off. A phase end, a group lost and a Defender flip during the probe end it without a hold.

### Tests (`BattleReconTests`, red first)

Candidate filter (player-owned, feud, company, moving parent, parent with posture move this tick, standing only, morale bar); stand point safety against a visible superior enemy (target and corridor) and not against a weaker one; stale entry point picked before stalest band before forward; repeat-target suppression; per-hour start bound on the doctrine clock; mission legs (confirmed only on an executor Issued outcome, Stop on first sight within stand-off, Return on superior contact, Recalled on a moved waypoint, timeout); probe nomination one per empty band, never for a Defender, never outside Probe, never linked / player-subtree groups, ends on visible contact with one Stop; look point bounded advance; zero allocation after warm-up. Ledger: entry stamps and stalest band. Director: zero enemy → Scouting; Scouting ResolveTask with zero enemy gives Probe/Screen and Hold for a DefensiveNetwork; a Defender (no contact or reconnaissance contact) gets DefensiveNetwork and no Probe task, Meeting/Attacker unchanged; GT recon screens for a Defender; a Defender's cavalry still scouts. Probe hold: weaker contact and arrival hold, superior contact does not, released on a reassigned ledger task, kept across a phase change with the same task, replaced by a new nomination, never overwritten by another group's hold; `HoldBlocks` only backward targets. Review round 2: the stance guard table; a Defender never committed offensively, after a commit-then-downgrade and with an offensive operation held from before; the doctrine guard over every shape × phase; GT recon with a visible enemy; the army stance reaching the ledger without the opportunity update, and the stance read placed before the flag gate (source order); facing Entry only for Screen/Probe/GuardFlank, regiments taking the bearing out of range, and the brigade pulse submitting a screen's turn but nothing for the holding tasks; the remembered-target and band fog filters. Skirmisher push decision (screen stance, contact, player-owned, range list). Cavalry follow square/officer inputs; fire control mounted evade; facing Entry.

PROVE-MODE: harness for every pure rule above (`BattleReconTests` plus the touched doctrine tests; red first, mutation checks on the safety test, the per-hour bound and the outcome confirmation). Runtime proof is the in-game smoke below (Session A items 2.15-2.18): the producers line with 12 producers, the recon and probe first-fire lines, `[OrderArbiter]` Recon activity, `TacticalRecon` rows, and the skirmisher line standing farther out.

### Smoke watch (added to the BC7 checklist, Session A items 2.15-2.18)

See `### BC7 smoke checklist`, items 1.4 (producers count=12) and 2.15-2.18.

**As built (BC9):**
- Commits: design `3792223` / `b143e10`; implementation `5e7f3dc`.
- Files: `Tactical/Battle/BattleRecon.cs` (pure: `ReconSettings`, `ReconPicture`, `ReconMemory`, `BattleRecon.Plan` / `IsScoutCandidate` / `StandPointSafe` / `LookPoint` / `AdvanceDirection` / `TryEntryBearing` / `SkirmisherPushMeters` / `IsSquare` / `IsOfficerTarget` / `BandWorldPoint`), `Tactical/Battle/Producers/ReconIntentProducer.cs` (runtime; `TryProbe`, `TryLookTarget`, `TryEntryBearing`, `CurrentSettings`), `Patches/SkirmisherScreenDistancePatch.cs` (#80). Ledger: `TacticalEnemyContactLedger.ObserveEntryPoints` / `EntryPointCount` / `TryGetEntryPoint` / `TryStalestBand` / `FrameHeading`; `ArmyEvidenceBuilder.ContactLedgerFor` (internal read accessor) and a pooled enemy entry list. Contracts: `UnitFlags.DetachedCompany` / `Mounted` / `Linked`, `UnitRecord.ParentRegimentId`, `IntentSource.Recon`. `TacticalUnitType.Officer`/`Excluded` renamed `Scout`/`Courier` (vanilla 4/5).
- **Direction before contact.** The snapshot front axis has no target until an enemy is seen (forward +z by default, BC2b), so a look point, a probe point and the forward recon point go toward the operation's primary objective (`PostureTargets.TryOperationObjective`, the ledger's objective) and no farther than it until the axis has a target (`BattleRecon.AdvanceDirection`).
- **Director scope.** The no-contact rule applies to a newly selected objective only: an operation already active on that objective keeps the shipped rules (a committed advance on an empty known map objective continues, `ShouldCancelCommittedContact`), so `OperationDirectorMatchesNormalizedCommittedMapObjectiveIds` stays green.
- **Mission legs.** Outbound is fail-closed (no executor Issued outcome by the next tick ends it, `not-issued`); the Return leg keeps re-submitting through executor cooldowns until it arrives or the mission times out (a scout left out because its return move was cooldown-skipped is the Withdrawal producer's if it comes under fire). Arrival = within 60 m, or a confirmed move that left the scout without a path. One mission per side at a time; a mission never starts in the tick another ended.
- **Look points replace the objective stand-off** for every Scout and Probe task of the posture and division-play pipelines (`PostureTargets.TryResolve`), the probe nomination's fixed point first; the cavalry follow's `RequestScoutLocation` (Scout task) therefore also resolves to a look point. With the flag off the shipped targets apply.
- **Facing Entry** applies to Screen, Probe and GuardFlank tasks only (`TacticalFacingPulseDoctrine.WatchesEntry`); review round 2 made it reach the game (decision 8): before it the producer's group-facing gate refused Screen/Probe and the regiment pulse failed `threat-out-of-range`, while holding tasks did take the Entry bearing.
- **Skirmisher screen height.** The pushed link target takes the terrain height from vanilla's `BattlefieldSetup.GetTerrainHeight` (`PostureTargets.Bfs`); the step is `GetAngleTerrainPosStatic`'s (forward of angle a = (sin a, cos a), 26891-26905). `Terrain` itself is not referenced (no TerrainModule reference in the build).
- **Review fixes (`fix(battle-core)` commit).** (1) Look points replace only the forward targets of a Scout/Probe decision (`BattleRecon.UsesLookPoint`: `DoctrinePrimaryTarget`, `ObjectiveApproach`); a close-engaged break-off (`DoctrineFallbackTarget`, `ShouldBreakOffRecon`) and a recovery path keep their own targets. (2) The executor's Stop dedupe (`OrderExecutor.IsDuplicate`) also looks below the group: `StopRegiment` goes to every unit on the group's level, so a probe group whose HQ stands while its regiments still walk is stopped, not skipped as Duplicate. (3) Fourth target kind `RememberedEnemy` (above). (4) A Retried outcome on the mission's move (the executor's ±30° retry) disables the waypoint-based recall for that leg, so the scout's own rotated retry is not taken for vanilla's re-lay. Tests (red first): `look points replace only the forward targets of a scout or probe` (compile), `probe stop is not swallowed while the group's regiments still walk` (Duplicate before the fix), `recon looks at the nearest remembered enemy when confidence fell after contact` (compile), `recon own rotated retry is not taken for a recall` (ended before the fix). Harness 2001 → 2005 PASS.
- **Verify round 3 (PASS; R3-F1 landed).** The runtime sites that pass the stance into the tested guards are source-pinned (`StanceCallSitesPinned`): #45's `sideStance` and its two uses, the node-planning `Build` call, the posture fallback `TryBuildSingle`, the director/doctrine ledger call, the one stance field and the decision-matrix telemetry. Re-planted r5 (coordinator `sideStance` = Meeting) and r6 (orchestrator `Build` passes Meeting): both now exit 134, as do the posture-fallback, ledger, default-scorer and matrix plants. Smoke item 19 added. Harness 2178 → 2179 PASS.
- **Integrator round 4 (default scorer cap).** Decision 12f. Test written first (`defender: #45's default sector scorer never returns attack stance`: red `a defender's weak point: defend, not attack: expected 2 got 3`), with a Meeting control for both branches; mutation (cap removed) exit 134. Harness 2177 → 2178 PASS.
- **Review round 3 (verify r2: R2-F1, R2-F2, q13/q14, integrator decisions).** Node tasks guarded where planned (`CommandNodeOperationsRuntime.Build` / `TryBuildSingle` take the stance; the orchestrator and the posture fallback pass `CurrentBattleStance`); #45's ledger stance capped at 2 for a Defender (`MapForGroupStanceScorer` third argument, from `PopulateResolvedDecisionsForSide`); the posture/leaf guard moved into the pure `PostureStanceGuard` with source-pin tests; one stance reader (the division play reads `CurrentBattleStance`; `SideDefending` deleted); charges left to vanilla's escalation (decision 12e). Main `4ba3805` merged (`SkirmisherScreenDistancePatch` takes #80; next #81). Tests (written first; red: `SingleMainEffort: main is AttackObjective`, `main weakPoint=True stance 3`, `rewritten`; the pins and the play reader did not compile or failed before the wiring): five in `BattleReconTests`. Mutation evidence (each exit 134): the posture-pipeline guard call removed (round-2 plant q13, now killed by `posture pipeline: PostureStanceGuard.Apply( in order`), the leaf guard call removed (q14, `leaf cascade: …`), the node-planning guard removed, the #45 cap removed, the order rewrite removed, the division play's stance read removed. Harness 2172 (after the merge) → 2177 PASS.

F2: decision 8 made real (brigade group facing for Screen/Probe on Entry, regiments follow, holding tasks refused the source). F3: remembered-target and band visible-filter tests. F4: `SkirmisherScreenDistancePatch` named a result override in AGENTS.md and the catalog. F5: `SeparateUnitsToGroups` infantry condition, entry stamps survive a frame turn. F6: grow-only hold store. Mutation evidence (each exit 134): director filter removed; Committed kept; doctrine-assignment guard removed; GT recon guard removed; FixEnemy unguarded; stance refresh a no-op; Entry allowed for holding tasks; regiment Entry range gate restored; brigade pulse with the old `ShouldFaceThreat(task)`; remembered-target filter removed (was a surviving plant); band visible filter removed (was a surviving plant); hold overwrite restored. ## BC10-BC12 — remaining battle findings (planned)

**Branch:** `feat/battle-next` from `8f532ae` (`feat/battle-recon`, BC9). `feat/battle-next` includes `main` `742d7e4`: BC9 (`c3f35b1`, #80), the campaign logistics fixes (#81-#91) and the W&L economy fixes (#92-#99). Each slice works on `feat/battle-next-bc<n>` cut from `feat/battle-next` and merges back in the order below; `./.worktrees/battle-next` needs `ln -s ../../refs refs`. **Inputs:** the 2026-10-15 research notes `battle-deploy.md`, `deploy-supply.md` § C and ranked items 8 and 11, `facing-flanking.md`, `terrain-awareness.md` (session scratchpad, summarised below with every anchor re-read on build 14703111); this plan's Global Constraints; root, `Tactical/` and `Patches/` `AGENTS.md`.

**Merge order and dependencies.**
```
base: main 742d7e4 (BC9 #80 and its Session A item 2.19; BC10's smoke items start at 2.20)
BC10 deployment and movement honesty   (BattleTerrainGrid, perceived flanking, executor via-points and immediate placement)
  └─► BC11 flank response and envelopment   (uses BC10's grid for the flank legs' water test, BC10's perceived Outflanked, BC10's via-point moves for the march leg)
        └─► BC12 terrain picture for planning   (builds on BC10's grid; edits PlayTemplateCatalog / PlayProjection / DirectChildAllocator after BC11)
              └─► BC13 battle AI level of detail   (reads BC11's ReceivedFire and maneuver state; written in parallel with BC12, merged after it: both edit PostureIntentProducer.cs)
```
- BC11 cannot start its executor work before BC10's contract edits merge (`BattleCoreContracts.cs`, `OrderExecutor(Runtime).cs`); its pure parts (`FlankFacing`, `EnvelopmentManeuver`) can be written against a fixture grid in parallel.
- BC12's builder and queries are pure and can be written in parallel with BC11; its consumer edits (line planner, director, play projection, allocator evidence) merge after BC11.
- **Stage G** (campaign planner) is campaign-only and runs in parallel. Shared files only: `Plugin.cs` (one or two `Tactical` binds per slice), `docs/patch-catalog.md`, `docs/handoff.md`, `MEMORY.md`. Patch ordinals and bug ids are assigned at merge to `main`. BC10 took #112 (`ReinforcementArrivalGuardPatch`) and BUG-TAC-022 on merging `main` `8099aaa` (its next free ordinal after the performance patches #109-#111); BC11-BC14 name their patches and bugs without numbers until their own merge. Each slice merges back to `main` in the order above (BC10, then BC11, then BC12).

**Rules that bind all three slices** (in addition to the Global Constraints):
- Every battle write goes through the order executor. Producers write nothing; they may read the snapshot and static public terrain (BC10's grid, BC12's picture, `NavMesh` queries with a query filter, a `Map`-layer raycast), never live enemy fields.
- Fog: terrain, objectives, entry points and deployment zones are public; enemies only as perceived (snapshot observations; a Visible observation's own `EnemyRecords` row may be read, BC6b precedent).
- A Defender (vanilla `GetBattleType` 2, `ArmyOrchestrator.CurrentBattleStance`) never gets attack doctrine: every new task or maneuver below passes `PostureStanceGuard` / `TacticalDecisionDoctrine.TaskForStance`, and BC11's envelopment is never planned for a Defender.
- **Formation targets are not BC10-BC13's to change** (BC14 changes them; see § BC14). The 2026-10-15 formation-layout research found that the posture pipeline re-forms most tasked groups to group formation 0 (Line) through `CommandFormationCorrection.TargetFormationForTask` (`Tactical/Operations/CommandFormationCorrection.cs:146-176`, used at `PostureIntentProducer.cs:504-506, 576-577`), removing vanilla's double line (vanilla deploys AI groups in double line, decompile 85750/85859), and a probable per-call tug-of-war between vanilla `AdjustGroupFormations` (5925-5927: double line for a stance-3 group with at least 4 infantry) and the #68 arbiter Postfix (`Patches/BattleCoreArbiterPatch.cs:35-40`). BC10-BC13 leave that as it is (a separate formation slice may follow) and depend on no particular formation being issued: BC10's column march sets group formation 4 itself and hands back to whatever the posture pipeline's formation target is; BC11's wheel and in-place refuse keep the group's current `groupformation` and read the layout slots vanilla built for it; BC12's terrain lines set frontage and position, not formation; BC13's slot trigger reads `LeafSlotTarget` for any formation. Tests that need a formation use a fixture value, never an assumed 0.
- Doctrine timers on `TacticalDoctrineTime`; per-battle state through `BattleScope.Register`; every new flag default `true` with a truthful description and a reader; no Transpiler; no Prefix-skip (the new Prefixes below, #60's position record and #112, never skip vanilla; #112's is a snapshot/restore steer of a vanilla input, listed for review).
